AI 估值疑慮引發波動，惟股市仍有多頭可期
2026 年 7 月
全球主要股市 6 月 （截至24 日） 報酬跌多漲少，與5 月新興亞洲及 AI 半導體題材主導下的多頭
格局形成鮮明對比，主要因為市場同時承受美伊衝突升溫、 Fed 新主席華許上任後鷹派轉向，以及
AI 類股估值偏高引發的修正壓力，風險資產動能熄火。以美元計價6 月表現觀察，MSCI 中國與拉丁
美洲指數分別下跌 5.9%及 4.5%，表現最差。中國主因房市持續疲弱與內需復甦不均，並受其對中東
原油進口的高度依賴拖累 ， 拉丁美洲則受前波累積漲幅較大後的獲利了結與美元期間走強雙重壓抑。

美股因 AI 估值疑慮再起而拉回，拖累MSCI 新興市場與新興亞洲分別下跌 1.3%。惟有 MSCI 印度逆
勢上漲 1.9%，主要受惠 6 月下旬國際油價回落帶動。整體而言，6 月股市回檔較像是高基期後的短
線修正，並未改變今年資金偏好新興亞洲、日本與科技成長市場的方向。
6 月（截至 24 日）全球債市呈現「利率類承壓、信用類抗跌」的結構性分歧。全球主權債券單
月下跌 1.03%，跌幅居首，主因華許於 6 月會議釋出鷹派訊號，18 位官員中有 9 位於點陣圖計入今
年內至少一次升息，使美國 10 年期公債殖利率一度升至 4.5%，存續期間較長之利率類債券遭逢重
估。全球投資級公司債下跌 0.67%，跌幅次之，亦受存續期間拖累。新興當地債小跌 0.12%，反映
因美元指數回升使來自匯率的貢獻由正轉負。信用類債券則表現相對 較佳，新興美元債券單月上漲
0.53%、亞洲高收益債上漲 0.49%領先，主要受惠信用利差續處歷史低檔、企業基本面強健且違約率
仍偏低、到期牆延後且供給以再融資為主。

近期股市雖因 Fed 升息疑慮及 AI 類股估值過高而修正，但美光財報及蘋果商品漲價均印證 AI
產品供需緊俏且企業資本支出依然強勁 。 市場近期以類股輪動方式進行修正 ， 顯示投資人對於整體股
市信心仍在。在投資策略上，短期不宜追逐高估值個股，但可於震盪時分批布局 AI 基礎建設、半導
體供應鏈及台日股市。
債市方面，當前美債提供高於過往均值的高殖利率，將可相當程度抵銷殖利率續揚的風險。然因
投資人對 Fed 後續貨幣政策存有疑慮，或導致殖利率曲線短端受升息預期而有較大波動；長端又有
期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險。因此，建議債券佈局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國
公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸引力的息收，若未來通膨預期放緩，在資本利得亦有表現機會。

美國股市：企業盈餘亮點集中 AI，股市評價尚屬合理，經濟成長趨緩但韌性猶存
美股第一季財報顯示，標普 500 成分股獲利有八成企業超越市場預期，整體每股盈餘同比增長
26.2%，創下 2021 年以來新高，企業盈餘上修動能強勁。隨運算需求從大模型訓練轉向推論，市場
資金快速轉向記憶體、光通訊、ASIC、CPU 與通用型伺服器供應鏈，引領板塊快速輪動。接下來第
二季 AI 產業仍將帶動資訊業獲利高增長，而能源、原物料產業第二季財報仍有不錯表現，但消費、
金融、醫療等，甚至通訊服務產業都有成長放緩跡象。
美國關稅談判正轉向以「301 法案」推進，目前針對「強迫勞動貨品進口禁令」，美國貿易代表

署（USTR） 已建議對全球60 個經濟體加徵 10%與 12.5%的額外關稅 。 預料下半年陸續將有更多301
調查結果公布，這將為川普關稅談判提供法源依據，迫使各國供應鏈加速赴美設廠，為美國製造業注
入投資動能，故對美國整體經濟表現無須看淡。
伴隨企業獲利預期上修 ， 標普500 指數前瞻本益比目前仍在 21.45 倍水準 ， 且通訊服務僅在20.9
倍、資訊科技僅在 24.8 倍，估值水平依舊合理。當前市場質疑重點在於AI 產業吸引大量資金投入資
本支出，故縮減企業發放現金股利與股票回購的能力，然而考量下半年仍有期中選舉行情可期，且
AI 產業變革快速，新題材層出不窮，操作上維持分批布局的建議。
美國經濟在 2026 年表現顯示成長趨緩但韌性猶存，2026 年第一季 GDP 成長率為 2.6%，表現
仍優於歐元區、日本等其他市場。失業率維持在低檔，5 月非農新增就業仍有 17.2 萬人，失業率持
平於 4.3%。5 月 CPI 年增率升至 4.3%， 核心CPI 年增率加速到 2.9%， 能源價格仍為推升通膨主因；

同月份 PPI 與核心 PPI 分別來到 6.5%與 4.9%， 顯示上游成本壓力正持續向下游轉嫁。勞動市場結構
性分歧，金融就業人數下滑，然而資料中心、半導體與電力等基礎建設投資，拉動非住宅營建業與製
造業僱傭需求，支撐整體就業數據，消費端方面，5 月美國零售銷售月增率回升至 0.9%，其中加油
站銷售與無店鋪零售商銷售(電商)表現強勁，前月走弱的汽車及零部件銷售逆勢反彈，顯示在K 型經
濟支撐下，高收入群體與特定消費仍表現穩健。
預料新任主席華許在確立聯準會改革方案前不急於升息，但目前利率仍處歷史高位，對房市、汽
車和消費造成壓抑 。 觀察密西根大學消費者信心指數在第二季來到有紀錄以來最低 ， 且美國新屋開工
下降，房市持續低迷，美國經濟仍需消化高利率與通膨帶來的負面效應。

歐洲股市：歐洲央行態度轉鷹，靜待美伊停火與關稅明朗化
歐洲央行在 6 月會議升息 1 碼，並持續警告中東地緣政治風險可能為通膨前景增添不確定性，因
此將今年通膨預期上調至 3.0%（前值 2.6%） ， 預計要到明年下半年才有機會回到 2% 的政策目標 。
不過，與 2022 年通膨高峰時期不同，目前歐洲就業市場並未出現明顯過熱，薪資壓力也未持續升
溫，因此我們認為本次升息後，歐洲央行更可能轉向觀望，利率期貨則顯示，市場仍預期年底前有再
升息 1 碼的可能性。
歐洲央行本次會議將邊際貸款利率、主要再融資利率及存款機制利率上調 1 碼至 2.65% 、

2.4% 與 2.25% ，會議中所有委員均同意升息。勞動力需求進一步降溫，薪資成長預計將在一年內
放緩，目前沒有看到與工資相關的二次效應，本次會議未討論中性利率和中性區間，如果不升息，通
膨因此失控 ， 重新控制將更加困難 。GDP 成長預估來看 ，2026 年爲 0.8%，2027 年爲 1.2%，2028
年爲 1.5% 。通膨方面，2026 年爲 3%，2027 年爲 2.3%，2028 年爲 2.0% 。核心通膨來看，
2026 年爲 2.5%，2027 年爲 2.5%，2028 年爲 2.2% 。
歐洲經濟成長動能放緩，但製造業維持擴張態勢，雖然歐洲央行將 2026、2027 年經濟成長率預
估值下調，自 0.9%、1.3%調整至 0.8%、1.2%。然 6 月歐元區綜合 PMI 自 48.5 回升至 49.5，顯示
景氣萎縮幅度正逐步收窄，製造業活動仍維持在擴張區間，同月服務業 PMI 雖僅 48.9，但也已釋出
跌勢趨緩訊號。
ECB 升息 1 碼對抗通膨風險，後續升息路徑分歧，受美伊衝突引發的油價影響，歐洲通膨壓力再

度上升，5 月歐元區 CPI 年增率已達 3.2%，為防範通膨失控，歐洲央行在 6 月利率決策會議將存款
利率從 2.0%上調至 2.25%。目前市場對歐洲央行後續的升息路徑看法顯著分歧，鷹派陣營認為 6 月
升息 1 碼後、第三季會再升 1 碼，並維持利率水平至 2027 年底；鴿派觀點認為隨油價回跌，短期內
再升息必要性降低，故後續仍將觀察各國通膨轉嫁能力。
待美伊戰事落幕、美歐關稅底定，歐股可望啟動估值修復，展望後市，若美伊停火協議落實、引
領國際油價回吐先前漲幅，將成為扭轉歐股當前弱勢、並吸引國際資金回流的催化劑。此外，美國改
採以 301 條款課徵關稅，對歐洲出口實質影響仍須觀察，惟預料美歐盟友關係穩固，終將透過談判
達成雙邊協議。目前泛歐 600 本益比以 2026 年獲利僅 15.6 倍。待短期不確定性因素陸續消退，市
場情緒可望改善，有利啟動估值修復行情。

日本市場：成長動能仰賴出口，政府補貼支撐景氣表現，股市屢創新高
GDP 動能放緩，受惠族群集中於AI，日本 2026 年第一季 GDP 年增率僅 0.4%，創下 2024 年走
出經濟衰退以來最疲弱表現，市場已將全年度 GDP 成長預估下調至 0.6%。然而 5 月出口年增來到
17%，創 2022 年 11 月以來最快增速，其中半導體相關出口年增逾六成、反映全球資料中心需求全
面爆發，推升記憶體晶片與電子零組件價格走揚，故受惠族群集中，下半年成長動能仍高度仰賴 AI
產業。
財政補貼壓低通膨 ， 油價回跌降低財政負擔，近期 ，首相高市早苗推行 「責任的積極財政」 政策，

透過實施電費、瓦斯補貼，並將全國汽油價格維持在每公升約170 日圓水平。未來隨補貼措施退場，
預期物價將出現階段性反彈。日本央行已於利率決策會議把本年度核心通膨預估上調至 2.8%。然而
在利率走高背景下，日本政府財政結構惡化，根據2026 財年政府預算規劃，國債利息支出將達總預
算的 10.65%，明顯高於前一財年的 9.13%。所幸美伊戰事有降溫跡象，預期日本政府將持續縮減刺
激方案規模。
日銀升息 1 碼，美日利差未收斂壓抑日圓表現，且日本央行於 6 月會議決定升息 1 碼至 1.0%，
主要考量國內物價上行風險加劇、薪資維持強勁成長動能，且經濟下行風險受控。市場普遍預期下次
升息落在 2026 年第四季。然而高市若為緩解財政壓力，可能干預日銀升息路徑。鑑於美日利差未出
現明顯收斂 ， 即使日本官方進場干預 ， 對日圓匯率的支撐效果恐怕相對有限 ， 日圓匯率預估於155-163
區間震盪。
日經指數近期屢創新高，6 月 22 日史上首度突破 72,000 點、連七個交易日刷新紀錄。上漲動能

來自日圓貶至 161 附近，顯著優化出口企業的盈餘表現，同時「實體 AI」投資計畫引爆半導體與 AI
類股行情 。 就2026 年以來 ， 領漲標的涵蓋東京威力科創 、 村田製作所 、 鎧俠 、 軟銀、 三菱UFJ 金融、
愛德萬測試、松下控股、三井住友金融、瑞穗金融、藤倉等。上漲族群主要為 AI 供應鏈以及受惠於
升息的金融族群，但即使過去一年日股飆升，目前以 2026 年獲利，本益比仍在 24 倍合理位置。
波克夏深化日本投資佈局，外資買盤同步加碼，波克夏對日本資產配置持續增加。自 2020 年對
伊藤忠、丸紅、三菱商事、三井物產、住友商事等五家各以略高於 5%持股起步，至 2026 年 5 月對
各家持股均已突破 10%， 顯示加碼日本仍是其核心策略 。 與此同時 ， 外資對日股買盤持續湧入 ，2026
年迄今(截至 6 月 20 日為止)外資買超日股的累計金額擴大至 10.2 兆日圓 ， 反映國際資本對日股後勢
依舊樂觀。

台灣股市：AI 需求支撐製造業 PMI 高檔擴張，暑假旺季與股市推升服務動能
當前臺灣經濟正處於生產擴張的黃金期。最新公布的總體經濟數據與半導體龍頭營收呈現全面共
振，印證全球 AI 資本支出（CapEx）並非流於故事題材，而是實質轉化為台廠的訂單與獲利。儘管
全球金融市場短期受到地緣政治干擾與美債殖利率波動影響 ， 引發台股在高檔震盪 ， 但主計總處大幅
上修 2026 年 GDP 預測至 9.64%，配合製造業 PMI 續擴張至 60.7%，顯示基本面強勁。我們認
為，台股高檔的回檔屬於健康的獲利了結與籌碼沉澱，長線多頭架構未變。建議採取拉回布局策略，

聚焦 AI 實體基礎設施與邊緣運算（Edge AI）雙主線。
國發會公布之 6 月製造業 PMI 報 60.7， 連續第 6 個月維持在 60.0%以上的擴張速度 ， 六大產業
中，僅食品暨紡織產業與交通工具回報緊縮。細項中，新增訂單與生產分別上揚至 60.4（前 59.6）
與 58.7（前 57.9） ，同時原物料存貨和客戶存貨分別回跌至 61.9（前 65.7）和 49.4（前 50.5），
顯示在 AI 伺服器、半導體與高階製造的強勁拉貨下，庫存水位仍屬可控。
5 月海關出口達 784.8 億美元（年增 51.7%） ，寫下連 31 紅紀錄。其中，資通訊產品出口年
增 75.2%，電子零組件出口創歷史新高。同時，5 月進口額創下 605.7 億美元歷史新高（年增
54.9%） ，核心物資多為半導體資本設備，依進口領先出口 1 至 2 季之歷史慣例，預示下半年出貨
動能將再創高峰。台積電 5 月合併營收達 4,169 億 7,500 萬元（年增 30.1%） 。先進製程與高階
封裝產能持續滿載，且透過定價調整順利維持毛利率在高檔，讓獲利能見度直接延伸至後續數年。

主計總處正式將 2026 全年經濟成長率（GDP）預測大幅上修 1.93 個百分點至 9.64%，創下
16 年來新高紀錄。第一季 GDP 初估更達 14.55%，創 48 年來單季新高。 總經結構分析顯示，
這波榮景是由「出口拉動投資、投資進而促進消費」的良性循環。全球主要雲端服務供應商（CSP）
調高資本支出，其新一代伺服器建置「必須向台灣採購」 。商品出口實質成長預估將帶動全年商品出
口總值達 8,945 億美元，其年增率創 50 年來最高水準，構成台灣 GDP 增長的關鍵引擎。
儘管台股指數在高檔震盪加劇，但在 9.64% 的 GDP 強勁基本面保護下，下檔支撐極度紮實。
隨著 COMPUTEX 後終端裝置陸續開出 ， 消費性電子將迎來升級週期 ， 可望讓下半年經濟加速升溫。
我們持續看多台灣股市。

中國股市：製造業需求回溫，然動能僅集中於高新技術產業與出口端
中國 6 月製造業需求回溫，動能集中新產業與出口端，製造業 PMI 為 50.3（前 50.0） ，較前
月回升，並連續四個月維持在擴張區間。主要動能來自新出口訂單回升至 50.1（前 48.6） ，以及新
訂單升至 51.2（前 49.9） ，皆較前月回升至擴張區間，顯示需求端及出口端表現亦有所回溫。生產
經營活動預期上升至 54.3（前 53.9） ，製造業前景保有一定韌性，細項觀察，6 月生產指數 51.4
（前 51.2） ，持續維持在擴張區間，本月在新訂單回升、庫存指數回落至 47.7（前 49.3）之下，新

訂單減庫存擴大至 3.5（前 0.6） 。 另外受到美伊衝突緩解影響 ， 主要原材料價格大幅回落至 54.2（前
60.5） ，外部壓力緩解。
產業方面，新舊動能延續上月分化格局。高技術製造業 PMI 升至 53.5（前 52.9） ，裝備製造業
升至 52.5（前 52.1） ，上升趨勢持續，相較之下，傳統高耗能產業 47.1（前 47.1） ，建築業商業活
動 49.0（前 48.8） ，仍維持在較弱格局。整體來看，美伊衝突緩解有效帶動中國製造廠商轉趨樂觀，
但多數仍集中在新產業及出口端，後續仍待觀察新產業能否有效帶動內需動能回升。
然而，內需數據尚未見回溫：中國第二季以來的消費、固定投資數據下滑，房地產未見起色。工
業生產年增長 4.5%，表現最為堅挺，主要受到政策性資本開支與科技硬需求的支撐。零售銷售5 月
單月年增率轉負， 即便有五一黃金周的短暫拉動，但節後服務消費迅速墜落顯示出居民消費依然保
守。固定資產投資年比下降 4.1% 。 這是過往作為壓艙石的投資首次跌破 4% 防線。民間固定資

產投資年比更劇烈下滑 7.1%。
整體來說，中國政府大規模刺激的可能性偏低，主要政策料是調降 LPR 或結構性定向降準。其
政策核心在於為中下游民營中小企業減輕債務壓力。現行溫和的企業設備更新與家電 「以舊換新」 政
策邊際效益遞減。為遏制消費滑入通縮螺旋，中央財政下半年將擴大赤字，直接補貼居民端，如發放
消費券、加大育兒養老津貼等等。中國總體經濟正處於重組期，缺乏向上突破的動能，但向下有國家
政策工具與新興硬科技的實質托底 。 房地產全面反轉或大眾消費復甦的機率偏低 。 雖政府政策扶植的
新科技產業仍有機會，但整體看法仍偏向保守。

印度股市：外部供給衝擊與內部基本面的對抗，看法中性
全球供應鏈受到中東戰事的實質干擾 ， 印度的前瞻景氣指標卻給市場帶來了巨大的驚喜 。 最新
公布的 2026 年 5 月 HSBC 印度綜合採購經理人指數由 4 月的 58.2 逆勢回升至 59.3，創下近
6 個月來的最高擴張紀錄。其中，服務業 PMI 由 58.8 顯著彈升至 59.8，主因是國內消費模式在
中東局勢干擾下成功「外轉內」 ，內需服務動能十分強勁；製造業 PMI 5 月最終值亦來到 55.0，高
於 4 月的 54.7。 雖然新出口訂單因中東衝突擴大而減速 ， 但國內基礎建設與製造業的 「預防性囤貨」

需求，成功支撐製造業維持在歷史平均水準之上。
然而，內需並無法完全沖刷外部供給側帶來的窒息感。印度高達 85% 的原油依賴進口，中東
戰事導致國際油價攀升至每桶近 120 美元，對印度財政與物價造成極大考驗。最新數據顯示，5 月
份消費者物價指數（CPI）年增率由 4 月的 3.5% 抬頭至 3.9%。這主要是受到汽車燃油價格凍結
政策結束的影響 。此外 ，比原油更長遠的隱憂來自於氣候 。 氣象機構預警有高機率出現乾旱 。 歷史上，
降雨不足與聖嬰現象會嚴重抑制糧食與作物產量 ， 豆類、 油籽等作物的受災風險顯著高於有灌溉保護
的水稻和小麥。彭博與野村等機構一致預警，這項氣候乾旱威脅將在 10 月農作物收成期引爆新一
輪食品通膨，預估將食品與飲料通膨推向 6.0%，並使下半年整體 CPI 衝破央行的容忍上限。
面對經常帳赤字擴大、國際收支（BOP）惡化與盧比貶值壓力，印度當局並未坐以待斃。首先
在貨幣政策方面，印度央行（RBI）於 6 月 5 日一致決議維持政策回購利率在 5.25% 不變，立場

維持「中性」 。央行認為盲目升息防禦匯率不僅無效，反而可能將能源衝擊轉化為內需重傷，因此決
策者選擇保留政策空間以依賴數據靈活決策。不過，經濟學家多數預期，隨著年底通膨風險逐漸壓過
成長擔憂，央行這項「按兵不動」的政策不會持續太久。 其次在財政工具方面，為保障佔人口半數
的農業人口並穩定農村經濟，政府宣布 2026/27 財年肥料補貼預算大增 20%，並階段性減免汽柴
油，支撐內需經濟。
印度市場目前正呈現明顯的「基本面強韌」與「評價面修正」的矛盾訊號。不可否認，5 月高
企的 PMI 與 1Q GDP 的暴衝證實其內部需求並未崩潰，而政府靈活的黃金關稅政策亦成功控制了
經常帳的惡化 。 然而 ， 中東戰事帶來的原油窒息感與下半年季風降雨不足 ， 是極難迴避的供給側利空 。
這兩項毒素預計將在第四季將通膨推向 6% 以上，進而逼迫央行轉為年底升息，更高的借貸成本屆
時將進一步抑制工業與內需需求，也讓股市有評價修正的壓力。因此，我們建議現階段應採取較為防
守的中長線投資策略，以在估值向下修正的震盪期中，鎖定長期結構性獲利。

拉美市場：巴西選舉風險與通膨預期升溫，後市應保守看待
6 月以來拉美市場主軸已由單純降息利多，轉為政治風險、通膨預期與央行動態三方拉扯。巴西
總統大選民調顯示魯拉領先幅度擴大，疊加巴西通膨預期與 Selic 利率路徑連續上修，使市場開始重
新評估 2026 年政策與財政不確定性，也使整體拉美股市表現持續承壓。
選舉方面，最新公布的 Genial/Quaest 民調指出，魯拉對波索納洛的領先幅度再度擴大，第一輪
由前次 39%對 33%，轉為本次 39%對 29%，魯拉領先差距升至 10 個百分點；第二輪亦由前次魯拉
42%對波索納洛 41%，轉為本次 44%對 38%，領先 6 個百分點。由於魯拉為較不受資本市場所青睞

的候選人，其當選可能性上升，預期將不利於巴西股市表現。
另一方面 ， 通膨預期惡化也使巴西降息交易持續降溫 。截至 6 月 15 日的巴西央行市場調查指出，
2026 年巴西通膨預期由 5.11%上修 19 個基點至 5.30%，持續高於 4.50%通膨目標區間上緣。2027
至 2029 年通膨預期亦全數高於 3.0%目標，顯示市場對通膨回到央行目標區間信心不足。
受此影響，市場對巴西政策利率路徑也持續轉鷹，2026、2027、2028 年底 Selic 利率預期分別
升至 13.75%、12.00%、10.25%，較前次的 13.50%、11.50%、10.00%再度上修。財政面同樣削弱
政策可信度，2026 至 2028 年基本財政餘額預期仍為 GDP 的-0.50%、-0.40%、-0.24%，名目財政
赤字 2026 至 2029 年亦維持超過 GDP 的 7%，顯示巴西財政赤字問題持續惡化。
貨幣政策方面，巴西央行 6 月 17 日全票決議降息 25 個基點，將 Selic 利率降至 14.25%，表面

上延續降息循環 ， 但其聲明與前瞻指引缺乏清晰度 ， 使市場更難判斷央行是否會對通膨預期重新升溫
採取足夠強硬態度。巴西央行通膨預測亦同步上修，2026 年底整體通膨預測由 4.6%升至 5.2%，自
由價格通膨預測在 2026 年第四季由 4.5%升至 5.3%，在通膨壓力持續升高的狀況下，巴西短期內進
一步降息空間已相當有限。
巴西以外拉美央行亦呈現類似政策兩難，智利央行 6 月 16 日宣布維持政策利率 4.5%不變 ， 即使
其 2026 年 GDP 成長預測降至 1%至 1.75%，但仍因油價與地緣風險推升短期通膨而選擇觀望。
展望後市，拉美市場在巴西大選風險升溫、通膨預期持續攀升的背景下，後市股市上行空間料將
受限，尤其全球股市近期受到 AI 題材帶動，拉美市場在缺乏成長敘事下，表現料將難以跟上全球指
數表現，故我們認為目前對拉美市場應保守看待。

科技類股：Token 經濟學與邊緣 AI 外溢效應，帶動科技股進一步走揚
當前全球科技股正處於由 AI 算力基礎設施引導的「結構性重新定價」新起點。首先半導體產值
將正式跨越破兆美元大關，市場對於「AI 泡沫破裂」的擔憂缺乏實質數據支持。超大型雲端服務商
（CSP） 具備強韌的資產負債表與 EBITDA 創造力，大舉發債並非盲目燒錢。「Token 經濟學」 打通
了算力消耗與商業變現的阻礙 ， 這些都是科技股的中長線利多。 雖然美國貨幣政策走向可能引發短線
估值震盪，但基本面依然偏多。
根據 IDC 最新報告，全球半導體市場規模正迎來歷史性的結構性跨越，2026 年全球半導體總營

收預估將達到 1.29 兆美元 ， 較 2025 年增 52.8%。 其中 ， 單是資料中心半導體營收就將達到 4,771
億美元，預計到 2030 年將達 8,432 億美元，獨佔市場半壁江山。2026 年全球記憶體營收暴增至
5,947 億美元，其中 DRAM 營收跳升至 4,186 億美元，年增率高達 177%。主因是 CSP 業者為
了搶奪 HBM 與高階 DDR 產能，願意支付極高溢價，推升整體板塊毛利率。
輝達執行長黃仁勳定調的 「Token 經濟學」 ，將技術敘事轉化為可量化的財務獲利模型，其核心
論點為 「Compute is revenue（運算就是收入） ，AI 工廠生產的實體商品就是 Token。 而產生Token
的數量就可以與資本開支互相匹配，進而由算力推估出資本開支的回報率。在 1GW 功耗限制下，
Blackwell 世代年營收約 300 億美元；進入次世代 Vera Rubin 晶片架構後，因運算密度拉升，相
同功耗下的年營收預估將暴增 5 倍至 1,500 億美元 ， 提供 CSP 業者持續加大資本支出的實質回報

率支持。
此外，隨著集中式雲端算力的建置跨過臨界點，AI 算力紅利正加速向終端裝置外溢。智慧型手機
與 PC 本地執行 AI 推論的需求已迎來全面爆發，Edge AI 正為半導體市場創造出全新的高價值需
求類別。邊緣 AI 擺脫了過往智慧終端僅比較出貨量的殺價競爭，轉向「高附加價值」的規格升級溢
價。長期而言，隨著 AI 代理（Agentic AI）在終端裝置的滲透率攀升，半導體將迎來新一波結構性
定價與穩健的增長動能。
短期來看，由於通膨數據具備黏性，市場正重新評估聯準會 （Fed） 貨幣政策再度升息的可能性。
此流動性緊縮預期直接推升了美債無風險利率 ，可能對高估值的科技權值股造成本益比階段性震盪修
正。然而，科技巨頭極其強韌的實質獲利能力足以消化估值衝擊。2026 年半導體跨越 1.29 兆美元
產值已成剛需，CSP 業者 EBITDA 預估增長 13%，配合黃仁勳 Token 經濟學的變現邏輯，獲利增
長將有效消化高本益比，建議可持續加碼科技類股。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
能源類股：荷莫茲海峽運量逐步恢復，油價料將承壓
受到美伊戰爭持續朝收斂方向前進影響，市場重新評估波斯灣出口恢復後的供給回補壓力；同
時，近期中國原油需求與進口動能明顯轉弱，使油市主軸由短線斷供風險，轉向中期供給過剩與需求
破壞，受此影響，6 月油價出現大幅回落，相關能源類股表現也出現承壓。
高盛報告指出，自美伊 6 月 17 日達成臨時協議後，荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣出口流量快速回升，中
東局勢於 6 月 27 日油輪襲擊後再度升溫前，波斯灣石油流量七日均值已達每日 1,800 萬桶，約為戰
前水準近 80%。其中荷莫茲海峽流量在協議後前 10 日增加每日 700 萬桶，6 月 24 日單日估算流量

一度達每日 1,300 萬桶 ， 約為正常水準60%； 若依過去兩週平均每日增加100 萬桶的恢復速度推估，
波斯灣流量最快可在 7 月初回到戰前每日 2,300 萬桶水準。不過，6 月 27 日油輪襲擊後，多艘超大
型油輪傳出掉頭返航 ，6 月 27 日至 6 月 28 日前後經荷莫茲海峽出口量一度降至每日 500 萬桶以下 ，
不到正常水準 20%， 顯示中東石油出口恢復方向雖明確 ， 但短期內仍取決於船東對海峽安全的信心。
另一方面，需求端壓力也有望加速全球石油供需平衡。IEA（國際能源署）6 月月報估計，第二
季全球石油供應缺口為每日 310 萬桶 ， 低於高盛估計的每日 500 萬桶 ， 主因 IEA 對石油需求破壞
的估計更高，IEA 預估第二季需求破壞量為每日 550 萬桶，高於高盛估計的每日 490 萬桶。需求
端疲弱原因主要來自中國需求下滑。彭博分析指出，中國 5 月原油需求急劇下降，原油進口量降至
每日 680 萬桶，較去年同期下滑 40%，主因包含荷莫茲海峽關閉期間造成的採購干擾，以及經濟

放緩帶來的需求破壞。進一步看至 2027 年，IEA 預估在供給年增每日 790 萬桶，以及部分需求疲
弱延續下，全球每日將出現 500 萬桶供給過剩，高於高盛預估的每日 320 萬桶，顯示中期油市風
險已由短缺轉向過剩。
受到整體油市轉往供給過剩影響，高盛將 2026 年第四季布蘭特原油價格預測由每桶 90 美元下
修至 80 美元，2027 年平均價格由 80 美元下修至 75 美元；西德州原油方面，2026 年第四季均價
預估由 85 美元下修至 75 美元，2027 年則由 75 美元下修至 70 美元。摩根大通、巴克萊、摩根士
丹利、瑞銀與滙豐也紛紛下修今明兩年油價展望，預估 2027 年油價均價介於每桶 63 至 85 美元；
其中巴克萊預期第四季可能出現每日 300 萬桶供應過剩，滙豐則預期短期將出現小型供應過剩。
展望後市，能源類股仍需觀察美伊臨時備忘錄落實狀況、荷莫茲海峽運輸恢復及中東增產狀況是
否順暢，然而當前市場更為關注停戰帶來的風險溢價收斂，故中短期內油價表現料將較為疲軟，在此

狀況下 ， 能源類股表現料將難以勝過由 AI 題材帶動的全球指數表現 ，故對後市我們亦維持保守看法。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：央行增持黃金趨勢未變，惟聯準會偏鷹不利金價表現
6 月以來黃金類股表現持續落後整體指數表現，主因受到美國聯準會態度偏鷹派影響，市場升息
預期上升，推升美元與美債殖利率走強，連帶提高黃金這類無孳息資產的機會成本，使金價與黃金類
股短線表現持續承壓。
6 月 FOMC 會議雖維持聯邦基準利率在 3.5%至 3.75%不變，但利率點陣圖中位數由 3 月時預估
2026 年底的 3.375%上修至 3.75%，同時 18 位提交預測委員中，有 9 位預期年內至少升息一次，8
位預期維持不變，僅 1 位預期降息 1 碼，導致會後市場升息預期明顯攀升。對黃金而言，升息預期

升溫將推升美元與美債殖利率 ， 並提高持有無息資產的機會成本 ， 尤其金價先前在美伊戰爭導致長天
期公債殖利率攀升的影響下，表現已相對疲弱，近期利率預期轉鷹更容易使短線獲利了結壓力升高。
另一方面，新任聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在本次會議中並未屈從政治面的降息壓力，反而重申
2%通膨目標並強調貨幣政策的獨立性，有助於強化市場對聯準會政策紀律的信心。此舉雖有助於提
升美國政策可信度，卻也將對黃金帶來不利影響；由於黃金的部分避險買盤，源自於市場對美國財政
負擔、政策獨立性及美元信用的疑慮，故一旦市場相信聯準會將維持堅定對抗通膨的立場，這類避險
需求便可能逐步減弱，也讓黃金類股失去美國政策性避險這項題材。
然而，儘管短線黃金逆風重重，但是在央行買盤方面，仍未見到全球央行對於增持黃金的看法有
所轉變。世界黃金協會所出版的《2026 年央行黃金儲備調查》顯示，89%受訪央行預期未來 12 個
月全球央行黃金儲備將增加 ， 雖低於2025 年的 95%， 但仍高於2024 年的 81%與 2023 年的 71%，

顯示多數國家央行對於持續增持黃金儲備仍具高度共識。就自身配置而言， 45%受訪央行預期未來
12 個月自身黃金儲備將增加，高於 2025 年的 43%與 2024 年的 29%，並創下 2019 年以來最高比
例，另有 54%預期維持不變，僅 1%預期下降，顯示各國央行黃金配置意願尚未出現明確轉向 態勢。
該項調查也指出，各國央行增持黃金的原因，主要仍集中在儲備多元化與風險分散。90%受訪央
行認為黃金在危機時期具有避險性 ，84%認為黃金具長期保值與通膨避險功能 ，83%認為黃金是有效
的投資組合分散工具，80%認為黃金屬於儲備多元化政策的一部分。由此可見，央行買金並非單純追
逐短線價格，而是與官方儲備安全性、幣別分散及危機應對需求有關。
展望後市，全球央行增持黃金的趨勢仍未改變，然而短線上，在降息利多消失、美元及美債殖利
率偏向上行，且聯準會獨立性疑慮降溫的背景下，金價表現料將受到壓抑，預期後市黃金類股表現將
於區間內震盪整理，目前對黃金類股後市中性看待。

### Page 12

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
6 月中的 FOMC 會議以 12–0 一致決議維持聯邦資金利率目標區間於 3.50%–3.75%， 並重申維
持充足準備金政策。然而，投資人所真正關注的內容在於會後所公布的經濟預測摘要(SEP)。在 6 月
點陣圖中，九位官員預計今年至少會升息 1 碼，其中六位預計至少升息 2 次。另有九位官員預計利
率維持不變或會降息 。2026 年底聯邦基金利率中位數由 3 月的 3.4% 上修到 3.8%，2027 年由 3.1%
上修到 3.6%；同時，2026 年 PCE 通膨預估由 2.7% 大幅上修至 3.6%，核心 PCE 由 2.7% 上修

至 3.3%。這代表 Fed 不只承認通膨反彈，也認為利率路徑需要重新往上定價。彭博對於 Fed 主席
開場談話的 NLP 情緒模型亦反映了鷹派傾向的變化。
華許在此次的會後記者會中雖重申聯準會對物價穩定與最大化就業雙重使命的承諾 ， 但針對已持
續五年多高於 2%目標的通膨問題，他強調委員會將毫不含糊地致力於恢復物價穩定。同時Fed 也將
維持銀行系統的充足準備金政策。他也指出，儘管面臨不確定性，目前經濟活動穩步擴張，生產力和
資本投資強勁，且就業市場保持穩定；本次會議發布了更簡短直接的政策聲明，取消了華許認為不合
時宜的 「前瞻性指引」 ，使得此次會後聲明用字大幅精簡 （由4/29 會議時的 341 字減至 130 字） ，
Warsh 本人也選擇不參與提交經濟預測摘要（SEP）。作為領導層上任後的重要革新，他也宣布在未
來兩週內正式成立五個獨立小組以分別對「溝通」、「資產負債表」、「數據來源」、「生產力／就
業」與「通膨框架」等議題進行改革，旨在打造一個適應時代需求且專注於未來的聯準會。

美國總體經濟環境現處於「經濟具韌性但通膨僵固」的微妙平衡。即便 FOMC 6 月會議立場偏
向鷹派，但仍約有半數委員認為年底前利率將維持現行水準。彭博指出，倘美伊停火協議得以維持，
通膨或於未來數月回落，部分 6 月預期升息的委員 9 月可望轉為按兵不動。元大投顧認為「年底前
維持利率不變仍是大概率事件」 ，因近期震盪部分是因市場「尚未適應 Warsh 新溝通風格」所產生
的過度反應。此外，高盛在 FOMC 會後發表的最新報告也指出即便  Fed「升息的可能性雖增加，
但仍非其基本預期」。
我們認為，當前美債已充分反映通膨上行風險，並提供高於過往均值的高殖利率，將可相當程度
抵銷殖利率進一步走高之風險。然因投資人對 Fed 的後續貨幣政策存有疑慮，或導致殖利率曲線短
端受升息預期變化而有較大波動；長端又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險，故
存續期過長亦非明智之舉。因此，建議債券佈局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供
相對具吸引力的息收，若未來通膨預期隨中東緊張情勢放緩，在資本利得方面亦可望有所表現。

In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$65,400mn (+1% MoM/+15% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), largely in line
with  our estimate at NT$201,379mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY), but slightly
below the consensus at NT$208,250mn (+14% QoQ/+39% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe the modest shortfall in 3Q26 revenue  vs. the market expectation
of ~10% QoQ, is due to some component supply shortages and the
customer's pull-in schedule.
• Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26 revenue to grow by double-digit % QoQ on
alleviated component supply and solid demand.
• Regarding the USITC Investigation No. 337, we believe near-term impact
should be manageable, but we will continue to monitor the developments on
this matter (see our report here).

• Stay OW.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical
solutions are gaining more attention too. The value proposition is to leverage the wide
and slow architecture for a relatively short transmission distance of 50-100m. It can
achieve 1-2 pJ/bit of power consumption vs. 5-10pJ/bit for CPO and NPO architecture. The

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

### Page 11

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

Solid state transformers on the Nvidia RVL for the first time
In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

architecture

According to Infineon, SST could represent an emerging >$1bn market opportunity by
2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Gigabyte (2376.TW): AI servers to drive 2H26 growth; Capex 
commitments remain strong; Neutral
20 October 2026 | 10:16PM CST
3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Oct-Dec on a 
YoY basis, driven by rising AI server racks, leading our 4Q26 revenues YoY to 
accelerate to 79% (vs. 60% / 41% YoY in 1Q26 / 2Q26). However, we expect revenue 
decline in Oct-Dec on a MoM basis which we attribute to AI servers model transition 
and the high base in Sep; nevertheless, we continue to expect revenues sequentially 
increase qoq in 4Q26E. We expect the stronger growth in 2H26 to be driven by 
ongoing AI spending, customers’ faster AI deployment, and Gigabyte’s expanding 
customer base and enhancing backlog visibility. Customers’ infrastructure readiness 
and components procurement should continue to improve in 2H26, supporting the 
procurement on AI servers. The company maintains strong capex commitment, and 
we expect 2H26 capex to stay at a similar high level vs. 1H26, leading 2026 capex to 
grow at 148% YoY to NT$2.9bn to capture the strong AI servers demand, and 
customers’ requirements on diversifying production sites (e.g., Gigabyte is across 
Taiwan, US, Europe, and Southeast Asia).

Earnings revision: We factor in Gigabyte’s Sep revenue and raise our 2026E net

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Gigabyte 
We are Neutral rated on Gigabyte with a 12-month target price of NT$492. Our TP is 
based on a 14.2x 2027E P/E which is based on PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s share 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery, 2) faster-/slower-than-expected gaming PC growth, and 3) 
faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
6426 統新 
(Apogee Optocom Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元 
      2 6 . 0 0  X 2 0 2 7  E P S

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

可望取得一定市場地位。

证券研究报告 
宏观经济点评 2026 年 10 月 17 日

9 月经济数据点评：生产偏强、需求渐进修复

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要
求，国内油气增储上产持续推进，为采矿业生产提供了支撑 。二是电热燃
水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加值增长 5.6%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。从实物产量看，核电、风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。9
月，服务业生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；一是数
字信息和生产配套型服务业继续保持较快增长。 信息传输软件和信息技术
服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数 分别增长
9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。住宿和餐饮业生产指
数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩张
区间；电信广播电视及卫星传输、货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均
位于 55%以上1。 
投资低位企稳。9 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，降幅与 8 月持
平。按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降
11.0%明显收窄；剔除房地产后，当月投资同比约下降 3.4%，降幅较 8
月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。季调环比增长 0.07%，是 3 月以来首次转正。 
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域 。9 月，制造业累计投
资下降 2.1%，降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。结构上看，一是正增行
业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行
业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶航空航天、计算机通信电子设
备制造业投资分别增长 15.8%和 9.6%，纺织、电气机械和通用设备制造
业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、航空航天器及设备、电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.2%、
12.5%和 7.6%。二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。农副食
品加工、食品制造、化工、医药、有色金属冶炼、金属制品、专用设备和
汽车制造业投资分别下降 2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、
8.9%和 6.3%。 
地产继续承压，价格端修复难抵销售走弱。9 月，地产投资累计同比下降
20.6%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.7 个百分点；按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资
下降约 26.3%，降幅较 8 月扩大约 0.8 个百分点。结构上看，一是地产销
售再次走弱，约束投资预期。 商品房销售面积和销售额累计分别下降
13.2%和 13.9%，降幅较 1—8 月扩大 1.1 和 0.9 个百分点；按累计金额反
推，9 月当月销售面积和销售额分别下降约 20.7%和 20.1%，较 8 月的下
降 14.8%和 12.1%明显走弱。东部地区销售额降幅由 12.6%扩大至
13.4%。30 大中城市商品房成交面积同比下降 10.9%，较 8 月扩大约 7.5
个百分点，其中一线城市增速由 18.8%降至 2.4%，二线城市降幅由
15.8%扩大至 22.7%，三线城市降幅基本持平。二是供给和资金端仍然偏
弱。新开工面积累计下降 24.9%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.1 个百分点，竣工面
积降幅由 23.7%扩大至 25.1%。房企到位资金下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 0.6

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
个百分点，其中，自筹资金降幅由 20.0%扩大至 21.4%；国内贷款和个人
按揭贷款降幅则分别小幅收窄至 33.1%和 22.1%。三是价格端有积极变
化。9 月，70 城新房和二手房价格同比分别下降 3.0%和 4.8%，降幅均较
8 月收窄 0.3 个百分点；环比看，新房降幅由 0.2%收窄至 0.1%，二手房
下降 0.3%，与上月持平。一线城市新房和二手房价格分别上涨 0.1%和
0.2%，二线城市新房价格由下降转为持平、二手房降幅收窄。 
基建投资继续放缓，项目端景气 有改善迹象。9 月，基建投资累计同比下
降 4.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.2 个百分点。一方面，部分传统基建领域仍
然偏弱。交通运输仓储和邮政业投资增长 1.9%，较前期回落 0.2 个百分
点；水利环境和公共设施管理业投资下降 12.8%，降幅扩大 1.8 个百分
点，其中水利管理和公共设施管理业均下降 12%以上。电热燃水投资下降
6.8%，降幅较前期小幅收窄。另一方面，项目储备和部分结构性领域出现
积极信号，但向施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。9 月，建筑业商务活
动指数由 46.9%升至 50.3%，从业人员和业务活动预期指数也有所提高。
信息传输、航空运输和水上运输投资分别增长 30.8%、13.8%和 13.5%，
交通、能源、数据等“六张网”相关领域仍有支撑。 建筑业新订单指数由
42.4%升至 45.7%，尚未回到扩张区间，指向前端项目需求有改善，但向
施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项，商品消费仍受汽车拖累 。9 月，社零同
比增长 1.1%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后
社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月加快 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，商品和餐饮
消费同步恢复。9 月，商品零售额增长 1.0%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；
餐饮收入增长 2.1%，提高 1.0 个百分点。从更广义的消费口径看，服务
消费仍是消费的重要支撑。三季度，社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长
2.6%，其中服务零售额增长 5.0%，明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%；网上
服务零售额增长 5.3%。通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额
保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主
要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零售额分别增长
1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点；家具、建筑装潢材料仍分
别下降 3.0%和 9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增
量支撑主要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造
业、外需和服务消费构成主要支撑。往后看，增量支撑线索有两方面。一
方面，以“六张网”和 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金
投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新型政策性金融工具资
金已经超过 1000 亿元2，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向政策
正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实
物工作量仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐
步显现。另一方面，服务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部
门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养

### Page 4

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 4 
老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望继续快于商品消费。

风险提示：外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、
政策资金投放节奏不及预期。

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 7 
1. GDP：实际增长边际回升 
实际 GDP 边际回升， 名义增长继续快于实际增长。三季度经济增长呈现三个特征，
一是实际增长较二季度有改善。 三季度实际GDP 同比增长 4.5%， 高于二季度的4.3%，
但仍略低于上半年平均水平；前三季度 GDP 累计增长 4.6%，较上半年回落 0.1 个百分
点。分产业看，实际 GDP 回升主要来自第二产业改善。第二产业增加值增速由二季度
的 3.0%回升至 3.4%， 第三产业由5.1%小幅升至 5.2%， 第一产业则由3.7%回落至 3.6%。
二是名义 GDP 继续快于实际 GDP，第二产业价格修复仍是重要支撑。 根据现价计算，
三季度名义 GDP 同比增长约 6.0%，较二季度提高 0.1 个百分点，高于实际 GDP 增速
1.5 个百分点。按名义与实际增速差近似估算，三季度 GDP 平减指数约为 1.5%，较二
季度的 1.6%略有回落， 指向整体价格修复仍在延续， 但没有进一步加快。 结构上， 第二
产业价格为主要支撑。二季度，第一产业平减指数由-3.3%回升至-1.7%，第三产业则由
1.3%回落至 0.7%， 第二产业平减指数约3.0%升至 3.6%， 其中工业由3.8%上升至 4.4%，
其表现与国际能源价格上涨以及 AI 产业链、 有色和电子设备价格较强基本一致。三是内
需端对经济增长的支撑边际回落，其中投资为最大拖累，外需端支撑边际增强。 支出法
看， 前三季度最终消费支出对经济增长的贡献率为45.7%， 较上半年回落0.3 个百分点；
资本形成总额贡献率为 28.3%，较上半年回落 7.8 个百分点；货物和服务净出口贡献率
为 26.1%，较上半年提升 8.2 个百分点。

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 8 
2. 生产：工业继续回升 
工业生产继续回升，采矿业和电热燃水业改善显著。9 月，规上工业增加值同比增
长 5.5%，较 8 月提高 0.3 个百分点，环比增长 0.6%。总量上看，出口对工业生产的拉
动延续。9 月，出口交货值同比增长 17.0%，较 8 月提高 5.9 个百分点。结构上看，工
业生产回升有两方面原因。一方面，制造业增加值维持韧性，高增长仍集中在装备制造
和高技术制造领域。9 月，制造业增加值增长 6.0%，较 8 月小幅回落 0.1 个百分点，但
仍高于整体工业增速。其中，高技术制造业增长 15.3%；金属制品、通用设备、专用设
备、 铁路船舶航空航天、 电气机械和计算机通信电子设备制造业分别增长 8.2%、8.5%、
12.1%、11.7%、8.1%和 16.4%。下游消费制造业总体保持正增。农副食品加工、食品
制造、 酒饮料和精制茶、 纺织、 医药和汽车制造业分别增长4.1%、7.3%、5.5%、3.2%、
4.9%和 6.7%。另一方面，拖累项增加值边际修复。一是采矿业由负转正，对工业生产
的拖累明显减轻。9 月，采矿业增加值由下降 1.4%转为增长 1.3%，其中煤炭开采和洗
选业降幅由 5.6%收窄至 3.3%，石油和天然气开采业增速由 6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解
部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要求，国内油气 增储上产持续推进，为采矿业
生产提供了支撑。二是电热燃水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加
值增长 5.6%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。 从实物产量看， 核电、 风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。 9 月，服务业
生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 一是数字信息和生产配套型服务业
继续保持较快增长。信息传输软件和信息技术服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓
储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。 住宿
和餐饮业生产指数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩
张区间； 电信广播电视及卫星传输、 货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均位于55%以
上。 
图5 采矿业规上增加值显著回升（%）

图6 规上工业增加值环比高于季节性（%）

3. 投资：延续回落 
投资低位企稳。9 月， 固定资产投资累计同比下降7.2%， 降幅与 8 月持平。按累计
金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降 11.0%明显收窄； 剔除房地产
后， 当月投资同比约下降3.4%， 降幅较8 月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。 季调环比增长0.07%，
是 3 月以来首次转正。 
图8 固定资产投资低位企稳（%）

### Page 10

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 10 
制造业投资小幅修复， 增长仍集中在少数优势领域。9 月， 制造业累计投资下降2.1%，
降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。 结构上看， 一是正增行业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制
造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶
航空航天、 计算机通信电子设备制造业投资分别增长15.8%和 9.6%， 纺织、 电气机械和
通用设备制造业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、 航空航天器及设备、 电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长20.2%、12.5%和 7.6%。
二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。 农副食品加工、 食品制造、 化工、 医药、
有色金属冶炼、 金属制品、 专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、
8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、8.9%和 6.3%。

4. 消费：改善相对明显，服务强于商品 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项， 商品消费仍受汽车拖累。9 月， 社零同比增长1.1%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月
加快 1.4 个百分点。 结构上看， 商品和餐饮消费同步恢复。9 月， 商品零售额增长1.0%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 餐饮收入增长2.1%， 提高1.0 个百分点。 从更广义的消费口
径看， 服务消费仍是消费的重要支撑。 三季度， 社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长2.6%，
其中服务零售额增长 5.0%， 明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%； 网上服务零售额增长5.3%。
通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消
费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零
售额分别增长 1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%， 降幅较8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点； 家具、 建筑装潢材料仍分别下降3.0%和
9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。

5. 展望：有望延续生产较强、内需修复格局 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增量支撑主
要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造业、外需和服务消费构
成主要支撑。 往后看， 增量支撑线索有两方面。 一方面， 以 “六张网” 和8000 亿元新型
政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新
型政策性金融工具资金已经超过 1000 亿元，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向
政策正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实物工作量
仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐步显现。另一方面，服
务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，
叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望
继续快于商品消费。

外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、政策资金投放节奏不
及预期。

投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

## Document 214: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

## Document 279: Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-28).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.
Exhibit 1: The Five Shocks of 2026: What Investors Expected vs. What They Got.
January 2026 Consensus September 2026 Reality The Shock
ENERGY Relatively contained energy costs Diesel above $6/gallon Energy shock—a new tax on the physical economy
INTEREST 
RATES Fed easing expected Fed raised rates 25 basis points in September to 3.75%-4.00% Rate shock—easing cycle goes into reverse
EARNINGS Solid but moderating profit growth S&P 500 Q2 earnings growth headed toward around 29+ Positive earnings shock—corporate America vastly outperforms
POLITICS Republican control of Washington 
Democrats lead 44%-37% in recent generic 
congressional polling; chamber control  
remains contested
Political shock—policy outcomes become much 
less certain

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.

weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 288: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1).

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories

balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year 
Percentage points contribution to growth by type of services spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort…

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).
